  git clone https://github.com/uutils/coreutils.git
  git clone https://github.com/axboe/fio.git
  git clone https://github.com/axboe/liburing.git
  git clone https://github.com/linux-test-project/ltp.git
  git clone https://github.com/mit-pdos/xv6-riscv.git
  git -C coreutils rev-parse HEAD; git -C fio rev-parse HEAD; git -C liburing rev-parse HEAD
Run only the repositories you need; these are starting instructions, not a claim that dependencies already exist.
For each program record: exact commit/version, uname -r, build flags, input bytes, stdout, stderr, exit code, repeat count.
If no failure reproduces, compare the documented affected tag or inspect the linked patch. Record NOT REPRODUCED, never fabricate evidence.

LINUX/C/FILESYSTEM/CONCURRENCY INTERVIEW: 459 QUESTIONS -> SOURCE-TRACED BUGS
Prepared 2026-11-12. Pure text. No CVE selection. No security-company bug sourcing.
This file contains 459 question-specific investigations mapped to 35 source cases.
There are NOT 459 separately verified real bugs. Some questions have no matching real ticket and are marked ADJACENT.
A tracked issue is evidence of a report, not proof that this particular laptop reproduces it.
LIVE = issue was open when inspected; HISTORICAL = fixed; LAB = intentionally incomplete curriculum; reports can have pending patches.
Numbers labelled REPORTED come from issue or university lab. Numbers labelled EXERCISE are test inputs, not measured results.
Never test destructive I/O, mount or hung kernel paths on a live filesystem. Use scratch image and VM.
Practice sequence: end-user action -> first executable line -> internal object -> expected state -> first divergent state -> angry user symptom.
Happy path ?: identify the contract. Unhappy path ??: identify first step that violates the contract.
Stop before implementing the fix: the interview exercise is to derive and verify the failure.

FIRST REAL, LOW-RISK BUGS
  A05: uutils mkdir concurrent EEXIST return status, 12 workers x 5000 rounds, scratch directory only.
  A08: LTP cgroup helper: invalid controller name -> null dereference before check, standalone user-space test.
  A25: fio generated 10MiB/8192B = 1280 blocks, 149 abnormal compressible blocks reported; write only temp file.
  A01: LLVM 127-byte C structure copy, inspect -O2 assembly, no privileged calls.
  A10: btrfs mkswapfile status mismatch, only in scratch filesystem/VM.
  A26: fio paired trim+write mismatch (3412 vs 3411), DESTRUCTIVE; loop device VM only.


COMPLETE NUMERIC REPRODUCTION: fio #2157 (NO SOLUTION)
The first executable operation is fio generating 10 MiB of data with 8192-byte blocks.
The end customer requests compression=0 to create an incompressible I/O payload.
The angry customer sees a compressed page despite that test setting, which invalidates a compression/storage benchmark.
Reported comparison: fio 3.19 gives 0/1280 pages with >=128-byte compression; fio 3.42 gives 149/1280.
Expected per-block length=8192 bytes. Reported smallest compressed page=7517 bytes.
Run in a new temporary directory, not on a device:
  mkdir -p /tmp/fio-bug2157 && cd /tmp/fio-bug2157
  /PATH/TO/BUILT/fio --name=job1 --filename=fio-output.bin --rw=write --bs=8k --size=10M --randseed=99 --randrepeat=0 --refill_buffers=1 --buffer_compress_percentage=0
Check output without assuming the report reproduces on this computer:
  python3 - <<'PYTEST'
  import zlib
  with open('fio-output.bin','rb') as f: data=f.read()
  blocks=[data[i:i+8192] for i in range(0,len(data),8192)]
  sizes=[len(zlib.compress(b,level=1)) for b in blocks]
  print('bytes',len(data),'blocks',len(blocks),'compressed_by_128',sum(x<=8192-128 for x in sizes),'minimum',min(sizes))
  PYTEST
Source: https://github.com/axboe/fio/issues/2157
Now trace: fio input arguments -> buffer generation -> each 8192B block -> compression measurement.
?: Which invariant does --buffer_compress_percentage=0 claim, exactly?
??: Why does the 3.19 vs 3.42 result differ? Inspect the generator source and its commit history.
Do not implement the solution until an affected version fails locally.

COMPLETE NUMERIC SOURCE TRACE: mkdir #16344 (NO SOLUTION)
  12 workers wait at one barrier. A single directory path is absent at time T0.
  Reported syscall trace for one worker:
     statx(path) = ENOENT
     mkdir(path,0777) = EEXIST
     statx(path) = S_IFDIR
     process exit = 0
  If one directory is created, exactly one worker is allowed to claim success in this fixture.
  Reporter observed 2653, 2726 and 3358 bad rounds out of 5000, versus 0 for GNU mkdir in same test.
  Source code and Python barrier harness: https://github.com/uutils/coreutils/issues/16344
  Inspect uutils source and run report harness on its 0.10.0 build in a scratch directory.
  ?: From the system call result, who actually created the directory?
  ??: Which user-visible exit code contradicts that syscall result, and why does this violate a lock invariant?

COMPLETE CONFLICT TRAIL: LLVM #86499
  C source contains struct { uint8_t x1[127]; } and two assignment forms.
  LLVM -O2 assembly from issue: temp form calls memmove@PLT, direct form emits memcpy-style operations.
  Initial reviewer argues same-pointer libc memcpy is undefined; reporter cites LLVM memcpy intrinsic allowing source==destination.
  Test both src=dst=2 and src=2,dst=3 in one array, then examine IR; do not assume memcpy and LLVM intrinsic have identical contracts.
  https://github.com/llvm/llvm-project/issues/86499

ISSUE EVIDENCE, SOURCE TRAIL, AND REPRODUCTION START POINT
Each A-number identifies one sourced public report or one explicitly marked teaching lab.

A01 LLVM emits memmove for a temporary 127-byte structure
What: When two elements in the same 127-byte array are selected, the regions are equal or disjoint. The report argues LLVM intrinsic memcpy is legal, while the generated call is memmove.
Why: trace the violated operation-level contract; do not assume a proposed patch is the only valid fix.
Where: Clang -O2 code generation; issue includes a C test and resulting assembly
Who: llvm/llvm-project code/test + the local caller exercising it.
When: Open issue at research time
Without: reproduce with the smallest temporary input; note permissions and version prerequisites.
Which numeric discrepancy: 127 bytes; compare a temporary-based copy and a direct copy; record assembly calls, not wall-clock claims.
Action: Create the C reproducer from the issue. Run clang -O2 -S -o - copy.c; search emitted calls for memcpy or memmove.
Call chain: C entry: Foo tmp = p[src]; p[dst] = tmp -> generated assembly. Compare direct p[dst]=p[src].
Laptop: No kernel access; C compiler only.
Restriction: Optimization defect, NOT evidence of data corruption.
Primary evidence: https://github.com/llvm/llvm-project/issues/86499
Check: compare issue contents with source at a recorded commit, and a test/patch when linked; do not infer a fix from the title.

QUESTION-BY-QUESTION RESEARCH MAP (ORIGINAL 459 QUESTIONS, NO SOLUTIONS)
Use the full original question; the primary issue represents a real codebase failure, not necessarily the exact question topic.
DIRECT: the cited report tests a key mechanism in this question. ADJACENT: it provides a traceable comparison case, not an exact bug in that feature.
Each entry retains seven W checks, numeric inputs, two entry points, and an actionable failure investigation.

Q001 [Whiteboard] Write a byte-by-byte implementation of memcpy. Which contract lets the implementation assume that source and destination do not overlap?
Bug: A01 [DIRECT]; secondary A02 [DIRECT].
What: question tests byte operations; reported case is LLVM emits memmove for a temporary 127-byte structure.
Why: When two elements in the same 127-byte array are selected, the regions are equal or disjoint. The report argues LLVM intrinsic memcpy is legal, while the generated call is memmove.
Where: Clang -O2 code generation; issue includes a C test and resulting assembly
Who: local caller + llvm/llvm-project component; compare with independent caller or reference version.
When: Open issue at research time; replay the affected tag first if the current build is fixed.
Without: compare normal and failing path without modifying production disks or applying a published fix.
Which: primary A01 (https://github.com/llvm/llvm-project/issues/86499); secondary A02 (https://github.com/llvm/llvm-project/issues/117332).
Number: 127 bytes; compare a temporary-based copy and a direct copy; record assembly calls, not wall-clock claims. Independent exercise: Probe n=0,1,9,29; use 0x1000/0x1005 and an overlapping region; check every destination byte.
Entry: Caller passes pointers and byte count -> C function -> machine copy -> caller sees final bytes.
?: happy path: what numeric state must stay true from caller to returned result?
??: failed path: at what FIRST source line does actual state cease to match it?
Steps: 1: Draw pathname or descriptor -> kernel object -> storage/result. 2: State expected object identity and byte count. 3: Use a scratch file/device; record stat, mountinfo, status, trace. 4: Compare one state transition at a time. Do not patch.
Check: Check data identity, returned byte count, exit code and whether one file object still exists after name change.

SOURCE AUDIT NOTES
Primary documents read during research include the bodies of actual GitHub issues, linked reproducer snippets, review/patch activity, and MIT lab assignments.
For exact code-line claims, consult the cited report and record the git commit before inspecting line numbers; lines shift across commits.
Reference source: Linux kernel overlayfs copy-up: https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/master/fs/overlayfs/copy_up.c
Reference source: util-linux mount propagation source: https://github.com/util-linux/util-linux/blob/master/sys-utils/unshare.c
Reference source: LTP regression tests: https://github.com/linux-test-project/ltp/blob/master/testcases/kernel/syscalls/listxattr/listxattr04.c
Reference course: MIT 6.1810 2026 kernel locks: https://pdos.csail.mit.edu/6.S081/2026/labs/lock.html
Reference course: MIT 6.1810 2026 filesystem limits: https://pdos.csail.mit.edu/6.S081/2026/labs/fs.html
FIXED or duplicated reports are preserved only as historical reproductions; do not claim a new upstream contribution for them.
Fio and liburing reports describe user-observed kernel or userspace behavior; source fault ownership is a separate question to establish.
For a real patch, require failing test on original commit, a causally justified change, test now passing, and no regression on unrelated inputs.
2026-11-12 source status is a snapshot and can change. Web report results are not measurements from your laptop.
Contrary source trail for LLVM #86499: reviewer objected to src==dst under ordinary libc memcpy; reporter pointed to LLVM memcpy intrinsic contract, which permits equal or nonoverlapping pointers. Read both replies before concluding compiler defect.
Contrary source trail for fio #2128: a patch initializes comp_time and prevents SIGABRT, but reviewer argues rate_iops_min still cannot fire when completions occur only in io_u_quiesce. Separate crash-fix from behavioral-correctness testing.
